(Note: In adolescents or adults, may be limited to feeling restless.)\n  manifestation: Sitting still for a long review feels physically uncomfortable, like pressure building inside me until I need to stand up, walk to the printer, or invent a reason to move.\n- key: S013\n  name: Quiet_Activity_Difficulty\n  description: Often unable to play or engage in leisure activities quietly.\n  manifestation: Even during things that are supposed to be relaxing, like reading at home or sitting quietly before a salsa class starts, I end up humming, checking something, or talking because pure quiet makes me antsy.\n- key: S014\n  name: Constant_Activity\n  description: Is often 'on the go', acting as if 'driven by a motor'.\n  manifestation: On weekends I tell my husband I'm going to rest, but then I bounce from laundry to email to a run to volunteer planning and don't really stop until I'm exhausted.\n- key: S017\n  name: Waiting_Turn_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty waiting his or her turn.\n  manifestation: Waiting in line at the courthouse records office or sitting in a clinic lobby makes me tense and impatient, and I keep checking my phone or rehearsing what I need to say instead of just waiting.\n- key: S018\n  name: Interrupting_Intruding\n  description: Often interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: I catch myself jumping into conversations before people finish, especially with junior auditors, because the answer or idea feels urgent in my head and I blurt it out before I can stop myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Even in elementary school, my report cards said I was bright but lost assignments, rushed past small details, talked out of turn, and needed reminders to finish work.
